### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-072

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character notices a small change in the setting and considers whether it signals a larger change in circumstances. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1421 examples from 6 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-073

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1422 examples from 7 settings over 10 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-074

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers examined the object alongside related examples from the same period before drawing a conclusion about its purpose. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1423 examples from 8 settings over 5 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-075

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The researchers considered both the average response and the spread of individual observations before drawing the broader conclusion. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1424 examples from 9 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-076

STATUS: LEGACY

SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2

SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1425 examples from 10 settings over 9 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-077

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest.

Passage 2: In examining how migratory birds alter stopover timing when food availability changes, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The evidence combines formal policy records with descriptions of how residents actually used the new arrangement. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 1426 examples from 3 settings over 4 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-078

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Several examples show the same broad tendency, although the feature serves a different role in each setting. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1427 examples from 4 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-079

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1428 examples from 5 settings over 8 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supporting",
  "have supported",
  "supports"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-080

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the acoustic design of small performance spaces. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1429 examples from 6 settings over 10 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-081

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1430 examples from 7 settings over 5 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-082

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A broader comparison shows that the recurring feature can serve more than one function. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1431 examples from 8 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-083

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1432 examples from 9 settings over 9 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-084

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character notices a small change in the setting and considers whether it signals a larger change in circumstances. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1433 examples from 10 settings over 4 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-085

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. Researchers compared records from several communities rather than treating one locality as representative of the whole region. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1434 examples from 3 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-086

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1435 examples from 4 settings over 8 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-087

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition.

Passage 2: In examining changes in how local newspapers described public libraries in the early twentieth century, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The researchers considered both the average response and the spread of individual observations before drawing the broader conclusion. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1436 examples from 5 settings over 10 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-088

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. An object that first appears incidental becomes relevant when the character considers what has changed around it. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1437 examples from 6 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-090

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1439 examples from 8 settings over 9 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-091

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1440 examples from 9 settings over 4 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-092

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. An ordinary detail is placed beside a later reflection so that the reader can infer a change in the character’s perspective. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1441 examples from 10 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-093

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13

SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2

SKILL: Transitions

QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1442 examples from 3 settings over 8 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-094

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A broader comparison shows that the recurring feature can serve more than one function. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1443 examples from 4 settings over 10 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-095

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis separated the broad pattern from the possibility that one unusually large reading affected the first comparison. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1444 examples from 5 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-096

STATUS: LEGACY

SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1445 examples from 6 settings over 7 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-097

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2

DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation.

Passage 2: An account of whether an imaging method detects canopy changes more consistently after calibration becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers compared records from several communities rather than treating one locality as representative of the whole region. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1446 examples from 7 settings over 9 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-098

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers examined the object alongside related examples from the same period before drawing a conclusion about its purpose. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1447 examples from 8 settings over 4 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-099

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1448 examples from 9 settings over 6 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supporting",
  "have supported",
  "supports"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-100

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1449 examples from 10 settings over 8 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-101

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 1450 examples from 3 settings over 10 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-102

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Several examples show the same broad tendency, although the feature serves a different role in each setting. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1451 examples from 4 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-103

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1452 examples from 5 settings over 7 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-104

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. An ordinary detail is placed beside a later reflection so that the reader can infer a change in the character’s perspective. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1453 examples from 6 settings over 9 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-105

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Researchers considered differences among local conditions before drawing a broader conclusion about the policy. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1454 examples from 7 settings over 4 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-106

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1455 examples from 8 settings over 6 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-107

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest.

Passage 2: In examining farmers adapting cooperative practices when regional prices became less predictable, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis separated the broad pattern from the possibility that one unusually large reading affected the first comparison. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1456 examples from 9 settings over 8 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-rw-108

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character notices a small change in the setting and considers whether it signals a larger change in circumstances. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1457 examples from 10 settings over 10 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

PROMPT:
A field researcher compares two measured states and reconstructs the unknown quantity from the reported relationship. The model provides the constraint needed to determine the target. The initial condition establishes the reference relationship. A linear model is y = 6x + 8. For what value of x is y = 86?

CHOICES:
[
  "13",
  "12",
  "14",
  "26"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
Subtract 8 and divide by 6 to obtain x = 13.

PROMPT:
A research coordinator compares linked measurements and uses the reported constraint to determine the result. The requested value follows from the relationship among the measured quantities. The initial condition establishes the reference relationship. A data set has first quartile 9 and third quartile 31. What is the interquartile range?
Enter your answer as a number.

[]

ANSWER: 22

EXPLANATION:
IQR equals Q3 minus Q1.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-math-math-module-1-m1-19

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: math
MODULE: math-module-1
DOMAIN: Problem-Solving and Data Analysis
SKILL: Weighted means
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A field researcher compares two measured states and reconstructs the unknown quantity from the reported relationship. The calculation uses the reported relationship directly. The initial condition establishes the reference relationship. Group A has 17 observations with mean 25; Group B has 34 observations with mean 33. What is the combined mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "29.33",
  "31.33",
  "30.33",
  "60.66"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
Use the weighted total divided by the combined number of observations.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-math-math-module-2-high-35

STATUS: LEGACY

TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: math
MODULE: math-module-2
DOMAIN: Advanced Math
SKILL: Quadratic parameter reasoning
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A resource planner compares a starting condition with a later condition to determine the requested quantity. The quantities are connected through the model given in the scenario. The selected comparison condition establishes the target relationship. For 5x² − 70x + k = 0, the equation has exactly one real solution. What is k?

CHOICES:
[
  "490",
  "246",
  "244",
  "245"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
A repeated root requires the discriminant to equal zero.

Substitute x = 16: y = 2(16) + 14 = 46.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-math-math-module-2-standard-57

STATUS: LEGACY

TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: math
MODULE: math-module-2
DOMAIN: Advanced Math
SKILL: Exponential equations
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A quality analyst checks how a reported change follows from the quantities given in the scenario. The calculation focuses on the condition that links the known and unknown values. The reported comparison condition establishes the target relationship. If 6^x = 7776, what is x?

CHOICES:
[
  "6",
  "5",
  "4",
  "10"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
Equal powers with the same base have equal exponents, so x = 5.

SECTION: math

DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A resource planner compares a starting condition with a later condition to determine the requested quantity. The calculation uses the reported relationship directly. The reported comparison condition establishes the target relationship. Group A has 23 observations with mean 27; Group B has 24 observations with mean 39. What is the combined mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "32.13",
  "34.13",
  "33.13",
  "66.26"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
Use the weighted total divided by the combined number of observations.

The reported comparison condition establishes the target relationship. A survey includes 587 responses. 22% select option A. How many responses select A?

EXPLANATION:
Multiply 587 by 22/100.

TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: math

DOMAIN: Algebra
SKILL: Linear functions and representations
DIFFICULTY: easy

PROMPT:
An engineering team represents the relationship symbolically and checks the unknown against an observed condition. The calculation focuses on the condition that links the known and unknown values. The observed comparison condition establishes the target relationship. A line passes through (13, 44) and (15, 48). What is its slope?

CHOICES:
[
  "3",
  "2",
  "1",
  "4"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
Slope = (48 − 44)/(15 − 13) = 2.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-13-math-math-module-2-low-87

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-13
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-13
SECTION: math
MODULE: math-module-2
DOMAIN: Geometry and Trigonometry
SKILL: Similarity and scaling
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A planning analyst models a changing quantity from a stated baseline and rate. The calculation focuses on the condition that links the known and unknown values. The observed comparison condition establishes the target relationship. Two similar figures have corresponding lengths in the ratio 5071:1. The smaller figure has area 50668. What is the larger area?

CHOICES:
[
  "1302929697389",
  "1302929697388",
  "1302929697387",
  "2605859394776"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
Areas scale by the square of the length ratio.

The observed comparison condition establishes the target relationship. A triangle has base 20274 and height 20269. What is its area?

CHOICES:
[]

ANSWER: 205466853

EXPLANATION:
Use one-half times base times height.
